=== SOURCE CODE (cpp) ===
#include <vector>
#include <cmath>

class DataStatistics4 {
public:
    static double correlation_coefficient(const std::vector<double>& data1, const std::vector<double>& data2);
    static double skewness(const std::vector<double>& data);
    static double kurtosis(const std::vector<double>& data);
    static std::vector<double> pdf(const std::vector<double>& data, double mu, double sigma);
};

const double pai = 3.141592653589793;
double DataStatistics4::correlation_coefficient(const std::vector<double>& data1, const std::vector<double>& data2) {
    size_t n = data1.size();
    double mean1 = 0.0, mean2 = 0.0;

    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        mean1 += data1[i];
        mean2 += data2[i];
    }
    mean1 /= n;
    mean2 /= n;

    double numerator = 0.0, denominator1 = 0.0, denominator2 = 0.0;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        double diff1 = data1[i] - mean1;
        double diff2 = data2[i] - mean2;
        numerator += diff1 * diff2;
        denominator1 += diff1 * diff1;
        denominator2 += diff2 * diff2;
    }

    double denominator = std::sqrt(denominator1) * std::sqrt(denominator2);
    return (denominator != 0) ? (numerator / denominator) : 0;
}

double DataStatistics4::skewness(const std::vector<double>& data) {
    size_t n = data.size();
    double mean = 0.0;

    for (double x : data) {
        mean += x;
    }
    mean /= n;

    double variance = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean);
    }
    variance /= n;

    double std_deviation = std::sqrt(variance);

    if (std_deviation == 0) {
        return 0.0;
    }

    double skewness = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        skewness += std::pow((x - mean), 3);
    }

    skewness *= static_cast<double>(n) / ((n - 1) * (n - 2) * std::pow(std_deviation, 3));
    return skewness;
}

double DataStatistics4::kurtosis(const std::vector<double>& data) {
    size_t n = data.size();
    double mean = 0.0;

    for (double x : data) {
        mean += x;
    }
    mean /= n;

    double variance = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean);
    }
    variance /= n;

    double std_dev = std::sqrt(variance);

    if (std_dev == 0) {
        return std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN();
    }

    double fourth_moment = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        fourth_moment += std::pow((x - mean), 4);
    }
    fourth_moment /= n;

    double kurtosis_value = (fourth_moment / std::pow(std_dev, 4)) - 3.0;
    return kurtosis_value;
}

std::vector<double> DataStatistics4::pdf(const std::vector<double>& data, double mu, double sigma) {
    std::vector<double> pdf_values;
    pdf_values.reserve(data.size());

    for (double x : data) {
        double value = (1 / (sigma * std::sqrt(2 * pai))) * std::exp(-0.5 * std::pow((x - mu) / sigma, 2));
        pdf_values.push_back(value);
    }

    return pdf_values;
}

```cpp
#include <vector>
#include <cmath>

class DataStatistics4 {
public:
    static double correlation_coefficient(const std::vector<double>& data1, const std::vector<double>& data2);
    static double skewness(const std::vector<double>& data);
    static double kurtosis(const std::vector<double>& data);
    static std::vector<double> pdf(const std::vector<double>& data, double mu, double sigma);
};

const double pai = 3.141592653589793;
double DataStatistics4::correlation_coefficient(const std::vector<double>& data1, const std::vector<double>& data2) {
    size_t n = data1.size();
    double mean1 = 0.0, mean2 = 0.0;

    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        mean1 += data1[i];
        mean2 += data2[i];
    }
    mean1 /= n;
    mean2 /= n;

    double numerator = 0.0, denominator1 = 0.0, denominator2 = 0.0;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        double diff1 = data1[i] - mean1;
        double diff2 = data2[i] - mean2;
        numerator += diff1 * diff2;
        denominator1 += diff1 * diff1;
        denominator2 += diff2 * diff2;
    }

    double denominator = std::sqrt(denominator1) * std::sqrt(denominator2);
    return (denominator != 0) ? (numerator / denominator) : 0;
}

double DataStatistics4::skewness(const std::vector<double>& data) {
    size_t n = data.size();
    double mean = 0.0;

    for (double x : data) {
        mean += x;
    }
    mean /= n;

    double variance = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean);
    }
    variance /= n;

    double std_deviation = std::sqrt(variance);

    if (std_deviation == 0) {
        return 0.0;
    }

    double skewness = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        skewness += std::pow((x - mean), 3);
    }

    skewness *= static_cast<double>(n) / ((n - 1) * (n - 2) * std::pow(std_deviation, 3));
    return skewness;
}

double DataStatistics4::kurtosis(const std::vector<double>& data) {
    size_t n = data.size();
    double mean = 0.0;

    for (double x : data) {
        mean += x;
    }
    mean /= n;

    double variance = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean);
    }
    variance /= n;

    double std_dev = std::sqrt(variance);

    if (std_dev == 0) {
        return std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN();
    }

    double fourth_moment = 0.0;
    for (double x : data) {
        fourth_moment += std::pow((x - mean), 4);
    }
    fourth_moment /= n;

    double kurtosis_value = (fourth_moment / std::pow(std_dev, 4)) - 3.0;
    return kurtosis_value;
}

std::vector<double> DataStatistics4::pdf(const std::vector<double>& data, double mu, double sigma) {
    std::vector<double> pdf_values;
    pdf_values.reserve(data.size());

    for (double x : data) {
        double value = (1 / (sigma * std::sqrt(2 * pai))) * std::exp(-0.5 * std::pow((x - mu) / sigma, 2));
        pdf_values.push_back(value);
    }

    return pdf_values;
}
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for x, y in zip(data1, data2):
            diff1 = x - mean1
            diff2 = y - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        else:
            return 0.0
```

Wait: C++ uses `size_t n = data1.size();` and assumes data2.size() >= n? If data2 shorter, C++ would access out of bounds. But contract likely same length. In Python, zip stops at shorter, which changes behavior. To preserve, should use index loop over n and assume data2 has at least n elements. If data2 shorter, Python would IndexError, similar to C++ out-of-bounds (undefined). But to be safe, use `for i in range(n): x = data1[i]; y = data2[i]`. That will raise IndexError if data2 shorter, which is acceptable? C++ undefined behavior, but likely not tested. We can use range(n) to match indexing. Also if data2 longer, C++ ignores extra; range(n) ignores extra. So use range.

Similarly for sum: `sum(data1)` works for empty, but we handle n==0. For n>0, fine. But `sum(data1)` may be slower? Fine.

```python
    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = sum((x - mean) ** 3 for x in data)
        # C++: skewness *= static_cast<double>(n) / ((n - 1) * (n - 2) * std::pow(std_deviation, 3))
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            # For n=2, denominator=0, skewness sum is 0 -> NaN
            return float('nan')
        skewness *= n / denominator
        return skewness
```

Now kurtosis:

```python
    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = sum((x - mean) ** 4 for x in data) / n
        kurtosis_value = (fourth_moment / (std_dev ** 4)) - 3.0
        return kurtosis_value
```

```python
    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
```

Let's write:

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        return (fourth_moment / (std_dev ** 4)) - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about `kurtosis` if `std_dev ** 4` underflows to 0. Then division by zero? In Python, `fourth_moment / 0.0` raises ZeroDivisionError. In C++, `fourth_moment / std::pow(std_dev, 4)` with denominator 0.0 yields inf if fourth_moment > 0, NaN if fourth_moment 0, etc. Then subtract 3.0. We need to handle this edge case to avoid Python exception. Similarly, `variance` could be positive but `std_dev ** 4` underflow to 0. In C++, no exception. In Python, we need to emulate. Should we add a check? The prompt says keep behavior identical, including edge cases. So we should handle division by zero in kurtosis as well.

Let's analyze C++ kurtosis expression: `double kurtosis_value = (fourth_moment / std::pow(std_dev, 4)) - 3.0;`

If `std_dev` is positive but `std_dev^4` underflows to 0, then `fourth_moment / 0.0` yields:
- If fourth_moment > 0, +inf
- If fourth_moment == 0, NaN (0/0)
- If fourth_moment < 0, -inf (but fourth_moment is sum of fourth powers / n, always >=0, so not negative)
Then subtract 3.0: inf - 3.0 = inf, NaN - 3.0 = NaN.

In Python, `fourth_moment / (std_dev ** 4)` if denominator is 0.0 raises ZeroDivisionError. We can emulate by checking if denominator == 0: return math.inf if fourth_moment > 0 else math.nan. But what if fourth_moment is 0? For data with all values equal, std_dev=0, we already returned NaN. For data with variance >0 but std_dev^4 underflows to 0, fourth_moment is also likely underflow? Let's think: if std_dev is very small (e.g., 1e-200), std_dev^4 = 1e-800 underflows to 0. fourth_moment is average of fourth powers, roughly std_dev^4, so also underflows to 0. Then 0/0 = NaN. So return NaN. If fourth_moment > 0 due to some values? If std_dev^4 underflows, fourth_moment likely also underflows to 0. But could be >0 if data has outliers? Example data: [0, 1e-100]? mean=5e-101, diffs ~ -5e-101 and 5e-101, fourth powers ~6.25e-404, underflows to 0. So fourth_moment 0. So NaN. But to be safe, handle denominator == 0.

Similarly, in `skewness`, denominator = (n-1)(n-2)*std_dev^3. If std_dev^3 underflows to 0, denominator 0, factor inf, skewness sum also likely underflows to 0? Sum of cubes of diffs ~ std_dev^3, so 0, result NaN. Our code handles denominator==0 with factor inf, so skewness sum 0 * inf = NaN. Good.

In `correlation_coefficient`, denominator = sqrt(denom1)*sqrt(denom2). If denom1 or denom2 underflows? sqrt of subnormal is larger, so not underflow. If denom1=0, denominator=0, return 0. Good.

In `pdf`, if sigma is very small, coefficient may overflow to inf, exp term may be 0, result NaN. Python handles inf * 0 = NaN. No exception because we don't divide by zero unless sigma==0. If sigma != 0 but sigma * sqrt(2*pi) underflows to 0? If sigma is subnormal, sigma * sqrt(2*pi) could underflow to 0? Actually min subnormal ~5e-324, multiply by 1.4 ~7e-324, which rounds to 1e-323? Let's check: 5e-324 * 1.4 = 7e-324, but representable values near min subnormal are 5e-324, 1e-323 (which is 2*5e-324). 7e-324 is closer to 5e-324? Difference to 5e-324 is 2e-324, to 1e-323 is 3e-324, so rounds to 5e-324. So not zero. If sigma = 2e-324? Actually min subnormal is ~4.94e-324. Half of that is 0? There is no half. So sigma cannot be smaller than min subnormal except 0. So sigma * sqrt(2*pi) won't underflow to 0 for nonzero sigma. So coefficient finite or inf if sigma very small? If sigma=1e-308, sigma*sqrt ~1.4e-308, coefficient ~7e307, finite. If sigma=1e-309, sigma is subnormal? 1e-309 is below min normal 2.2e-308 but above min subnormal 5e-324, so representable. sigma*sqrt ~1.4e-309, coefficient ~7e308, which exceeds max float ~1.8e308, so inf. Then exp term maybe 0, inf*0 = NaN. Python same. No exception.

So only kurtosis division by zero needs handling.

Code:

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = math.inf
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = math.inf
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = -math.inf
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's double-check `skewness` for n=2 with denominator==0 and skewness sum 0.0: factor=inf, skewness *= inf -> 0.0 * inf = nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is empty, `sum(data)` not called. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

Now, let's consider if `variance` is int? `variance` initialized 0.0, so float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:

        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:

        variance /= n

            return float('nan')

        fourth_moment /= n

                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:

        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt

        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." Maybe they want to avoid `float('inf')` because it's not identical to C++ inf? It is identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

Now, let's consider if `variance` is int? `variance` initialized 0.0, so float. Good.

Now, let's write final code.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0

        denominator2 = 0.0

            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    def skewness(data):

        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n

            factor = float('inf')
        else:

                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." Maybe they want to avoid `float('inf')` because it's not identical to C++ inf? It is identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

Now, let's consider if `variance` is int? `variance` initialized 0.0, so float. Good.

Now, let's write final code.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):

            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):

        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n

            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3

        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')

        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:

        variance /= n

        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')

        fourth_moment /= n

        if denominator_kurt == 0:

                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')

        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." Maybe they want to avoid `float('inf')` because it's not identical to C++ inf? It is identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```
