```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n

        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')

            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):

        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0

        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):

        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:

                ratio = float('-inf')

        result = []
        for x in data:

        return result

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." Maybe they want to avoid `float('inf')` because it's not identical to C++ inf? It is identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:

        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

Now, let's think about if `denominator` in skewness is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=2. We set factor = inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is 0 due to underflow and n>2? denominator == 0, factor = inf. Good.

Now, let's think about if `denominator` is 0 due to `(n-1)*(n-2)` being 0 for n=1? We returned earlier because std_deviation == 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `skewness`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `kurtosis`: return nan. Good.

Now, let's consider if `n` is 0 in `correlation_coefficient`: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma=0: list comprehension over empty returns []. Good.

Now, let's think about if `data` is empty and `pdf` with sigma!=0: result empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a tuple, `data[i]` works. Good.

Now, let's consider if `data` is a list of ints, `sum(data)` returns int, `mean` float. Good.

Now, let's think about "idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior." We used loops instead of comprehensions for variance etc. That's fine. We could use `sum((x - mean) ** 2 for x in data) / n` but that would create generator and sum, same behavior. But loops are okay.

Now, let's consider if `sum(data)` for large data with floats might have different rounding than C++ loop? Python's `sum` uses left-to-right addition, same as C++ loop. Good.

Now, let's think about if `variance /= n` in Python with n int and variance float: same.

Now, let's consider if `skewness` denominator `(n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)` in Python: `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is int, `std_deviation ** 3` is float, product float. If `(n-1)*(n-2)` is large, int converted to float. In C++, `(n - 1) * (n - 2)` is size_t, then multiplied by double, size_t converted to double. Same.

Now, let's consider if `n` is very large and `(n-1)*(n-2)` overflows size_t? In C++, size_t overflow wraps, but n is vector size, cannot exceed SIZE_MAX. In Python, int arbitrary precision. But for realistic sizes, same. Not an issue.

Now, let's finalize.

But wait: The prompt says "Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior." Maybe they want to avoid `float('inf')` because it's not identical to C++ inf? It is identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` should use `n = len(data1)` and then if `n == 0` return 0.0. In C++, if n=0, mean1 becomes NaN, but denominator=0, return 0. So yes.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 empty: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `correlation_coefficient` with n=0 and data2 non-empty: C++ would still return 0.0 because denominator=0. Our code returns 0.0 without accessing data2. Good.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=0: return 0.0. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with n=0: return nan. Good.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma=0 and data non-empty: return list of nan. C++ would compute `1/(0*sqrt)` = inf, `exp(-0.5 * ((x-mu)/0)^2)`. If x != mu, `(x-mu)/0` = inf, square inf, -0.5*inf = -inf, exp(-inf)=0, inf*0 = NaN. If x == mu, 0/0 = NaN, exp(NaN)=NaN, inf*NaN = NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's consider if sigma is -0.0. In Python, `sigma == 0` True, returns NaN. C++ with sigma=-0.0: `sigma * sqrt(2*pi)` = -0.0, `1 / -0.0` = -inf, `(x-mu)/-0.0` = -inf or +inf, exp(-inf)=0, -inf*0 = NaN. If x==mu, NaN. So all NaN. Good.

Now, let's think about if sigma is a very small negative number, e.g., -1e-320. `sigma == 0` False, coefficient = 1 / (-1e-320 * sqrt) = -inf, exponent same as positive sigma, exp(negative large) = 0, value = -inf * 0 = NaN. In C++, same. Good.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma negative and x-mu = 0: exponent -0.5*(0)^2 = 0, exp(0)=1, coefficient negative, value negative. C++ same.

Now, let's think about if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=0: coefficient = 1/(-1*1.414) = -0.3989, exponent 0, value -0.3989. Python same.

Now, let's consider if `pdf` with sigma = -1, mu=0, x=1: exponent -0.5*(1/-1)^2 = -0.5, exp=0.6065, value -0.242. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `skewness` with n=2 and denominator==0, we set factor=inf. But what if `std_deviation ** 3` is inf? denominator inf, factor = n/inf = 0, skewness sum maybe inf, inf*0 = NaN. Our code: denominator != 0, factor = n / denominator = 0.0, skewness *= 0.0. If skewness sum is inf, inf * 0.0 = NaN. If skewness sum finite, finite * 0 = 0. C++ same. Good.

Now, let's think about if `kurtosis` with `denominator_kurt` inf: ratio = fourth_moment / inf = 0 if fourth_moment finite, or NaN if fourth_moment inf. C++ same. Python `inf / inf` = nan, `finite / inf` = 0.0. Good.

Now, let's consider if `correlation_coefficient` with denominator inf: numerator / inf = 0 if numerator finite, NaN if numerator inf. C++ same. Python same.

Now, let's think about if `data` contains `float('nan')` and `sum(data)` returns nan. Good.

Now, let's finalize.

One more thing: In C++, `std::pow((x - mean), 3)` for `(x-mean)` negative and exponent 3 uses `pow(double, int)`? Actually there is overload `pow(double, int)` since C++11? It returns double. For negative base and integer exponent, result is negative. Python `**` same.

Now, let's think about if `data` is a list of ints and `(x - mean)` is negative, `(x - mean) ** 3` returns float because mean is float. Good.

```python
import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_dev = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_dev == 0:
            return float('nan')
        fourth_moment = 0.0
        for x in data:
            fourth_moment += (x - mean) ** 4
        fourth_moment /= n
        denominator_kurt = std_dev ** 4
        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:
                ratio = float('nan')
        else:
            ratio = fourth_moment / denominator_kurt
        return ratio - 3.0

    @staticmethod
    def pdf(data, mu, sigma):
        if sigma == 0:
            return [float('nan') for _ in data]
        coefficient = 1.0 / (sigma * math.sqrt(2 * math.pi))
        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
```

import math

class DataStatistics4:
    @staticmethod
    def correlation_coefficient(data1, data2):
        n = len(data1)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean1 = sum(data1) / n
        mean2 = sum(data2) / n
        numerator = 0.0
        denominator1 = 0.0
        denominator2 = 0.0
        for i in range(n):
            diff1 = data1[i] - mean1
            diff2 = data2[i] - mean2
            numerator += diff1 * diff2
            denominator1 += diff1 * diff1
            denominator2 += diff2 * diff2
        denominator = math.sqrt(denominator1) * math.sqrt(denominator2)
        if denominator != 0:
            return numerator / denominator
        return 0.0

    @staticmethod
    def skewness(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:
            variance += (x - mean) * (x - mean)
        variance /= n
        std_deviation = math.sqrt(variance)
        if std_deviation == 0:
            return 0.0
        skewness = 0.0
        for x in data:
            skewness += (x - mean) ** 3
        denominator = (n - 1) * (n - 2) * (std_deviation ** 3)
        if denominator == 0:
            factor = float('inf')
        else:
            factor = n / denominator
        skewness *= factor
        return skewness

    @staticmethod
    def kurtosis(data):
        n = len(data)
        if n == 0:
            return float('nan')
        mean = sum(data) / n
        variance = 0.0
        for x in data:

        fourth_moment /= n

        if denominator_kurt == 0:
            if fourth_moment > 0:
                ratio = float('inf')
            elif fourth_moment < 0:
                ratio = float('-inf')
            else:

        result = []
        for x in data:
            result.append(coefficient * math.exp(-0.5 * ((x - mu) / sigma) ** 2))
        return result
